- **paidf-curation-and-retrieval**: Use when operating PAIDF Curation and Retrieval or NVIDIA Cosmos Curator pipelines (split, filter, caption, embed, dedup, shard, image annotate) or PAIDF Data Mining nearest-neighbor matching on Curator embeddings. Activate for Make or CLI pipeline config, GPU run preflight, FFmpeg sidecar, SAM3 keys, or Curator-to-TAO handoff. Do not use for generic ETL, vector-database RAG, model training, orchestration, or embeddings outside Cosmos Curator and PAIDF Data Mining. - **paired-end-duplication-rate-evaluation**: Use when you need to estimate or reproduce the duplication rate of paired-end FASTQ data (e.g. in.R1.fq.gz/in.R2.fq.gz) and want the memory-efficient key/array method fastp uses rather than a hash-table approach. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/paired-end-duplication-rate-evaluation))
- **paired-end-fastq**: Handle paired-end FASTQ files (R1/R2) using Biopython. Use when working with Illumina paired reads, synchronizing pairs, interleaving/deinterleaving, or filtering paired data. ([swaruplab/operon](https://github.com/swaruplab/operon/tree/HEAD/protocols/sequence-io-paired-end-fastq) · ★104 · 2 copies)
- **paired-end-fastq**: --&gt; ([mdbabumiamssm/LLMs-Universal-Life-Science-and-Clinical-Skills-](https://github.com/mdbabumiamssm/LLMs-Universal-Life-Science-and-Clinical-Skills-/tree/HEAD/Skills/Sequence_Analysis/sequence-io/paired-end-fastq) · ★36 · 3 copies)
- **paired-end-fastq**: Keep R1/R2 reads in lockstep — synchronize iteration, filter pairs together, interleave, and deinterleave — using Biopython and the 2026 best practices. ([pradyumnasagar/open-research-skills](https://github.com/pradyumnasagar/open-research-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/bioinformatics-sequence/paired-end-fastq) · ★8)
- **paired-end-fastq-generation-from-alignments**: Use when an analysis needs FASTQ reads recovered from a subset of alignments in a coordinate-sorted, indexed BAM (e.g. reads mapped to putative Ig loci) so the reads can be fed to a de novo transcriptome assembler such as Trinity. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-fastq-generation-from-alignments))
- **paired-end-fastq-generation-from-bed**: Use when scReadSim has produced synthetic read-coordinate BED files (e.g., combined gene/inter-gene BED from scRNA_CombineBED) and you need actual paired-end FASTQ reads for downstream aligners or UMI-deduplication benchmarks. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-fastq-generation-from-bed))
- **paired-end-fastq-mate-handling**: Use when mapping paired-end shotgun metagenomic FASTQ reads to a protein-reference gene database (e.g., UniRef90) and you need per-mate alignment before downstream -hit filtering and gene counting. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-fastq-mate-handling))
- **paired-end-fastq-mate-synchronization**: Use when raw paired-end shotgun viral metagenomic FASTQ files (R1/R2) need adapter removal, low-quality base trimming, and host/contaminant read removal before viral identification, and mates must stay in register. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-fastq-mate-synchronization))
- **paired-end-fastq-merging**: Use when preparing paired-end RNA-seq unmapped reads (e.g., TCGA BAM reads failing alignment to hg19 and phiX NC_001422) as viRNAtrap input, since viRNAtrap treats each input FASTQ file as a distinct sample. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-fastq-merging))
- **paired-end-fastq-quality-control**: Use when raw paired-end Illumina FASTQ (e.g. NextSeq PE150, NovaSeq) needs quality control, adapter removal, and filtering before downstream analysis, and speed or a single-pass all-in-one tool matters. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/paired-end-fastq-quality-control))
- **paired-end-fastq-read-handling**: Use when input data are gzip-compressed paired-end FASTQ short reads (e.g., the SRR15859208 E. coli NDM5 dataset) and a k-mer distribution or Bloom filter input is needed before k/b selection for ntEdit+Sealer. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-fastq-read-handling))
- **paired-end-fragment-extraction**: Use when STAR-aligned paired-end scRNA-seq BAMs (e.g., Smart-seq2 data like gray mouse lemur testes cells) must be converted to strand-specific interval fragments before groHMM TAR discovery, because groHMM consumes read coverage intervals rather than raw BAM alignments. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-fragment-extraction))
- **paired-end-illumina-read-simulation**: Use when building synthetic FASTQ benchmarks from known viral reference genomes (e.g. BASV, BKV, HIV-1) to evaluate contig extension or assembly accuracy under varied read length, error rate, and depth conditions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-illumina-read-simulation))
- **paired-end-insert-size-distribution-filtering**: Use when profiling metagenomic read mappings against reference genomes and needing to discard improperly paired or aberrant-insert-size reads before computing coverage, ANI, or SNVs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-insert-size-distribution-filtering))
- **paired-end-metagenomic-read-preprocessing**: Use when reconstructing full-length SSU rRNA genes (16S/18S) from paired-end Illumina metagenomic FASTQ to enable species-level taxonomic profiling via RiboTaxa's dual-assembler strategy. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-metagenomic-read-preprocessing))
- **paired-end-naming-convention-application**: Reference for setting up a NEAT project with paired-end sequencing data where reverse-read FASTQ files must be linked to their forward mates inside the centralized Targets.txt file. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-naming-convention-application))
- **paired-end-overlap-analysis**: Use when assessing duplication levels of high-depth (e.g. ~10000x cfDNA) paired-end Illumina FASTQ data, or when deciding whether a tool's duplication metric accounts for read1/read2 pairing. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/paired-end-overlap-analysis))
- **paired-end-overlap-graph-assembly**: Use when you have a FASTA of mixed single- and paired-end viral reads (e.g. TAR-VIR recruited_reads.fa) and need to reconstruct strain-level haplotypes de novo, without a complete reference genome. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-overlap-graph-assembly))
- **paired-end-overlap-match-probability-computation**: Use when scoring overlapping paired-end FASTQ reads (e.g. 108-base Illumina reads) to decide which assembled sequences pass a quality threshold such as p = 0.9 or p = 0.6. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-overlap-match-probability-computation))
- **paired-end-overlap-scoring**: Use when merging overlapping paired-end Illumina FASTQ reads (e.g. 16S rRNA amplicons) where naive perfect-match overlap fails due to uncalled (N) or miscalled bases, and error-correcting probabilistic assembly is needed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-overlap-scoring))
- **paired-end-read-alignment**: Use when metagenomic FASTQ (paired-end WGSIM-simulated or real shotgun) reads must be mapped to a bacterial reference set to produce unique/split read BAMs for HGT breakpoint detection. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-alignment))
- **paired-end-read-alignment-bwa**: Use when running Anchored-fusion or its benchmark against de novo fusion callers: paired-end FASTQ (R1/R2) must be mapped with BWA-MEM (&gt;=0.7.17) to a target-gene fasta and genome fa (hg38/hg19) with matching GTF. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-read-alignment-bwa))
- **paired-end-read-alignment-to-reference**: Use when paired-end RIMS-seq FASTQ reads must be aligned to a reference genome — either user-supplied (option 2) or de novo assembled with SPAdes (option 1) — as a prerequisite for methylase specificity discovery. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-read-alignment-to-reference))
- **paired-end-read-alignment-to-reference-genome**: Use when synthetic scRNA-seq reads exist as unaligned FASTQ files (e.g., minnow output) and must be placed in genomic coordinates on mm10 to compare coverage against an aligned real BAM. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-read-alignment-to-reference-genome))
- **paired-end-read-assembly**: Use when Illumina paired-end FASTQ (or SAM/BAM) amplicon reads overlap and must be merged into full-length sequences with mismatch/uncalled-base correction, e.g., 16S rRNA libraries where naive assembly discards ~40% of reads. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-assembly--4aace21ef51e1d92))
- **paired-end-read-assembly**: Use when raw paired-end Illumina reads (e.g. SRA FASTQ like ERR008613) must be converted into genome assembly contig FASTAs for comparison with QUAST/WebQUAST. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-assembly--a5035386db5a753c))
- **paired-end-read-group-organization**: Use when a multi-sample sequencing workflow must ingest raw FASTQ inputs from a sample sheet into per-read-group QC and trimming steps, distinguishing paired-end (R1/R2) from single-end files for DNA-seq and RNA-seq. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-read-group-organization))
- **paired-end-read-local-reassembly**: Use when a draft scaffold FASTA contains N-wildcard gap regions between contigs and a paired-end short-read dataset (e.g. Illumina GAGE PE reads) is available to fill those gaps by local reassembly. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-local-reassembly))
- **paired-end-read-mapping-to-multi-genome-reference**: Use when a metagenomic analysis needs paired-end shotgun reads mapped against many reference genomes simultaneously so junction read pairs spanning two different references can seed donor/receptor detection. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-mapping-to-multi-genome-reference))
- **paired-end-read-merging**: Use when starting from per-sample paired-end gzipped FASTQ amplicon reads (e.g. Sample1_1.fq.gz/Sample1_2.fq.gz) and you need merged, primer-trimmed, quality-filtered amplicons before dereplication and OTU/ASV inference. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-merging--ab07c7402e44b94f))
- **paired-end-read-merging**: Use when raw paired-end amplicon FASTQ reads have been demultiplexed into per-sample files and must be merged and quality-filtered before OTU clustering or ASV denoising. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-merging--ab17ce572e203e05))
- **paired-end-read-merging**: Use when raw paired-end FASTQ reads from stool metatranscriptomes must be joined into longer single fragments (longer than 100 bp) before annotation, as the SAMSA pipeline aligns paired-end files after Trimmomatic cleaning. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-merging--063adc5fe8d7694c))
- **paired-end-read-merging**: Use when raw paired-end metatranscriptome RNA-seq data in FASTQ form must be converted into a single merged, trimmed, rRNA-filtered FASTQ before DIAMOND annotation, as in the SAMSA2 preprocessing step. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-merging--a2cb7b274000643e))
- **paired-end-read-overlap-assembly**: Use when processing paired-end, 3'-end tag scRNA-seq data (e.g. Microwell-seq FASTQ from Mouse Cell Atlas / Human Cell Landscape) where reads must overlap and be merged to recover the full insert containing the 3' cleavage site. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-read-overlap-assembly))
- **paired-end-read-pair-categorization**: Use when raw paired-end ChIA-PET (or HiChIP/PLAC-seq) FASTQ reads must be scanned for the bridge linker junction and partitioned by linker/tag content before read mapping. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-read-pair-categorization))
- **paired-end-read-pair-integrity-checking**: Use when a paired-end QC/trimming step (e.g. fastp) has produced cleaned_1.fastq.gz and cleaned_2.fastq.gz and you must confirm the pair is synchronized, non-empty, and valid gzip FASTQ before hybrid assembly. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-pair-integrity-checking))
- **paired-end-read-pairing-handling**: Use when classifying paired-end FASTQ read pairs (e.g. simulated 2x250 nt Illumina reads) with Kaiju, or when benchmarking throughput where read-pair ordering and naming conventions must be preserved. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-pairing-handling))
- **paired-end-read-primer-trimming**: Use when raw paired-end amplicon FASTQs (e.g., dual-indexed 181 bp COI reads from SRA PRJNA518082 Libraries A-D) still carry primer sequences and must be trimmed before denoising or OTU clustering. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-primer-trimming))
- **paired-end-read-quality-trimming**: Use when raw paired-end FASTQ reads (e.g. from SRA accessions like ERR008613) must be quality-checked and low-quality ends removed before assembly, with surviving mate pairs kept as FASTQ.GZ. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-quality-trimming))
- **paired-end-read-quantification**: Use when you have paired-end RNA-seq reads (FASTQ) and a reference transcriptome (FASTA), and you need to estimate transcript-level expression (NumReads and TPM). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v1/skills/paired-end-read-quantification))
- **paired-end-read-separate-clustering**: Use when processing paired-end 16S FASTQ reads with highly overlapping mates where quality drops in the overlap region make read merging lossy, and you want annotated OTUs without a merging step. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-separate-clustering))
- **paired-end-read-simulation-to-single-end**: Use when deciding whether paired-end sequencing is necessary for metatranscriptome annotation, or when quantifying how much annotation information is lost by processing paired-end reads as single-end. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-simulation-to-single-end))
- **paired-end-reverse-complement-handling**: Use when classifying paired-end reads (e.g., setB2_1/setB2_2 FASTA files) whose strand orientation is unknown, and classification quality depends on considering forward and reverse-complement strands. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-reverse-complement-handling))
- **paired-end-rna-seq-alignment**: Use when performing a targeted fusion search on paired-end RNA-seq FASTQ data by anchoring a gene of interest, and reads must be aligned to both the target gene transcript fasta and a whole-genome reference. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-rna-seq-alignment))
- **paired-end-short-read-alignment**: Use when you have paired-end FASTQ files (e.g. in.R1.fq.gz/in.R2.fq.gz) that must be mapped to a reference genome (hg19) and compared on alignment quality metrics across preprocessing variants. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/paired-end-short-read-alignment))
- **paired-end-similarity-matrix-computation**: Use when classifying paired-end metagenomic reads with LiME and you must build similarity matrices for all four collections (Reads1F, Reads1RC, Reads2F, Reads2RC) merged against the reference genomes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-similarity-matrix-computation))
- **paired-end-splicing-label-derivation**: Use when building an evaluation read set with definitive splicing ground truth from paired-end 10X scRNA-seq data (e.g., mouse/human scRNA-seq) to benchmark splicing-status predictors via AUC. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-splicing-label-derivation))
- **paired-end-strand-and-mate-handling**: Use when classifying paired-end metagenomic reads (e.g. 100 bp Illumina mates from SRR1804065 or setB2) with LiME, when strand direction is unknown and ambiguous reads must be resolved with mate information. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-strand-and-mate-handling))
- **paired-end-structural-variant-calling**: Use when analyzing sorted/indexed Illumina paired-end BAM/CRAM alignments (e.g. bwa mem-aligned HG002 WGS at ~20-40x) to detect SVs/indels ≥30 bp, or when benchmarking PE SV callers against a GIAB truth set with svbench. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-structural-variant-calling))
- **paired-end-support-threshold-filtering**: Use when a consensus VCF of SV calls (merged across callers with SURVIVOR) must be down-filtered before manual curation — e.g. dropping calls with low paired-end support or breakpoints in low-complexity or annotated LTR regions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-support-threshold-filtering))
- **paired-end-tag-alignment-and-bedpe-construction**: Use when processing raw ChIA-PET paired-end FASTQ data after linker trimming and you need aligned PETs as deduplicated BEDPE pairs for downstream peak and loop calling. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-tag-alignment-and-bedpe-construction))
- **paired-end-tag-fraction-computation**: Use when processing ChIA-PET fastq data through ChIA-PET2 and you need to quantify per-step PET retention (pre-/post-alignment, post-duplicate-removal, intra-chromosomal &gt;=4 PETs) for quality control. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-tag-fraction-computation))
- **paired-evaluation-execution**: Prepare or audit paired evaluate-gsf trials with frozen common inputs, independent evaluator pins, complete attempt accounting and separately authorized native execution. ([wfcosta/SoftwareFactory](https://github.com/wfcosta/SoftwareFactory/tree/HEAD/.gsf/skills/paired-evaluation-execution) · ★6)
- **paired-flag-multi-instance-registration**: CLI pattern for registering N instances of a composite config (e.g. spec+baseUrl pairs) using repeated paired flags and a pending-state parser. ([kjuhwa/skills-hub](https://github.com/kjuhwa/skills-hub/tree/HEAD/skills/cli/paired-flag-multi-instance-registration))
- **paired-flotation-water-route**: Create original short videos using the 双脚浮具水面路线｜交替承重、连续尾流与远端抵达 Creative DNA for MiniMax H3 or Seedance 2.0. Use when the user wants this causal, camera, motion, rhythm, or payoff structure with new subjects and surfaces. ([Wanfeng1028/muse](https://github.com/Wanfeng1028/muse/tree/HEAD/source/skill/minimax-h3-prompt-skill-t8-paired-flotation-water-route) · ★5)
- **paired-hardware-lifecycle-contract**: **Domain:** System architecture / device integration **Applies to:** Any project that pairs a host (phone, watch, laptop) with a peripheral (BLE accessory, USB device, HID controller, AR/VR display, hearing aid, dock). **Origin:** AR-Runner ADR 2026-05-19 — "BLE link is user-managed, not workout-scoped." ([jkrilov/AR-Runner](https://github.com/jkrilov/AR-Runner/tree/HEAD/.squad/skills/paired-hardware-lifecycle-contract))
- **paired-image-graph-training-data-preparation**: Use when preparing training data for GILoop: the input is a KR-normalized intra-chromosomal Hi-C contact map (or Juicer-dump O/E matrix) and paired image and graph samples are needed for dual-branch loop-calling training. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/paired-image-graph-training-data-preparation))
- **paired-inference**: Route Pix2Pix-Turbo paired image-translation inference for edge-to-image, stochastic sketch-to-image, custom Pix2Pix checkpoints, and local paired Gradio demos. ([VectorSpaceLab/AREX-Skill](https://github.com/VectorSpaceLab/AREX-Skill/tree/HEAD/skills/repositories/repo-skills/img2img-turbo/sub-skills/paired-inference) · ★357)
- **paired-insertion-counting-strategy**: Use when you have loaded fragment data from single-cell ATAC-seq experiments into a backed AnnData object (with fragments stored in .obsm['fragment_paired'] or . ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v1/skills/paired-insertion-counting-strategy))
- **paired-model-comparison-significance-testing**: Use when comparing a new causal-inference or prediction model against baseline configurations on the same candidate peak-gene pairs, and you need per-configuration retrieval AUROCs plus significance statistics for the differences. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-model-comparison-significance-testing))
- **paired-multiomics-data-preprocessing**: Use when you have raw paired scRNA-seq/scATAC-seq count matrices (AnnData objects) that must be normalized and feature-processed before training or evaluating a scButterfly cross-modality translation model. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-multiomics-data-preprocessing))
- **paired-multiomics-tnse-visualization**: Use when you need qualitative visual confirmation that translated single-cell profiles (predicted scRNA-seq or scATAC-seq from a cross-modality translator) cluster by cell type and match true labeled profiles. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-multiomics-tnse-visualization))
- **paired-nonparametric-hypothesis-testing**: Use when two configurations of the same method (e.g. weighted vs unweighted decoupleR runs) yield paired scores over identical dataset/regulator combinations and a directional significance claim must be tested nonparametrically. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-nonparametric-hypothesis-testing))
- **paired-nonparametric-method-comparison**: Use when two or more metabolomics normalization methods must be compared on identical GC-MS feature-intensity data with paired per-metabolite precision (RSD) values, to decide which method yields the highest coverage of compounds passing a &lt;30% RSD threshold. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/paired-nonparametric-method-comparison))
- **paired-observation-alignment**: Use when two score vectors cover the same methods/entities measured under different conditions or modalities and you must drop methods missing in either modality before computing a paired correlation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/paired-observation-alignment))
- **paired-omics-bgc-metabolite-linking-workflow**: Use when you have paired genomic and metabolomic data from the same microbial strains and want to link biosynthetic gene clusters (BGCs) to the metabolite features they plausibly encode — mine BGCs from assembled genomes with antiSMASH, tokenize BGC domains with iPRESTO/Pfam and cluster them into gene cluster families (GCFs) with BiG-SCAPE, build an MS/MS molecular network from paired LC-MS/MS data with GNPS to obtain molecular families (MFs), then score GCF-MF co-occurrence across strains (NPLinker-style Metcalf/hypergeometric scoring) to produce a ranked table of candidate BGC-metabolite li… ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/workflows/paired-omics-bgc-metabolite-linking) · 2 copies)
- **paired-omics-bgc-spectrum-link-validation**: Use when a paired omics (genome + metabolomics) dataset contains curated validated genome-metabolome links and you must quantify how many survive Metcalf and NPClassScore filtering, or verify prioritisation/rank improvements of known links. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/paired-omics-bgc-spectrum-link-validation))
- **paired-omics-data-retrieval**: Use when a linking or benchmarking analysis needs paired genome+LC-MS/MS samples with validated BGC–metabolite links, e.g. from the PoDP or a GNPS job task, to build fingerprints or evaluate precision/recall. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/paired-omics-data-retrieval))
- **paired-omics-data-structure-mapping**: Use when when you have a paired omics project document (JSON format) that combines MS/MS mass spectrometry data with genome identifiers, biosynthetic gene cluster information, sample preparation, extraction method, and instrumentation metadata, and you need to verify it conforms to the platform's. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/paired-omics-data-structure-mapping) · 2 copies)
- **paired-omics-dataset-configuration**: Use when a paired genome–metabolome dataset with a Paired Omics Data Platform (PoDP) accession (e.g. MSV000078836, MSV000084950, MSV000084771) must be assembled in NPLinker for BGC–MS/MS spectrum linking. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/paired-omics-dataset-configuration))
- **paired-omics-dataset-curation**: Use when building a genome-mining workflow that needs matched genome (or metagenome) and metabolome data for the same samples, e.g., to train or query NPOmix against PoDP paired datasets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/paired-omics-dataset-curation))
- **paired-omics-fingerprint-vector-analysis**: Use when you have paired genome/metagenome + LC-MS/MS samples and need to link MS/MS spectra (GNPS reference or cryptic) to biosynthetic gene clusters with confidence scores. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/paired-omics-fingerprint-vector-analysis))
- **paired-omics-metadata-retrieval**: Use when an analysis needs systematically curated BGC–metabolite linkages: e.g. assembling validated paired datasets for NPLinker scoring, benchmarking link-ranking algorithms, or finding projects by genus, extraction method, or metadata ontology terms. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/paired-omics-metadata-retrieval))
- **paired-omics-precision-benchmark-evaluation**: Use when NPOmix KNN predictions (GCF labels assigned to query MS/MS spectra) must be scored against validated BGC-metabolite links from MIBiG/PoDP to report top-1 and top-10 precision. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/paired-omics-precision-benchmark-evaluation))
- **paired-omics-project-filtering**: Use when the analysis goal is to find PoDP projects by extraction or sample-preparation metadata (e.g. projects that used methanol to extract cell pellets) via keyword search over project documents. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/paired-omics-project-filtering))
- **paired-probe**: Refuses a verdict a check did not earn: runs it with the fault present and absent and blocks when both agree; catches zero-sample sweeps and swallowed errors. Use before reporting any status, audit or nothing-found result. ([yonatangross/orchestkit](https://github.com/yonatangross/orchestkit/tree/HEAD/plugins/ork/skills/paired-probe) · ★305 · 3 copies)
- **paired-prompt-ui**: **Status:** Draft (extract after NumberDrill Phase 2 Wave 2 ships) **Context:** Blazor activity pages that require comparing two items side-by-side **Origin:** NumberDrill Disambiguate-the-system sub-mode (2026-05-04) ([davidortinau/SentenceStudio](https://github.com/davidortinau/SentenceStudio/tree/HEAD/.squad/skills/paired-prompt-ui) · ★31 · 2 copies)
- **paired-read-concatenation-for-classification**: Use when classifying paired-end reads from simulated or real metagenomes (e.g., HiSeq, MiSeq or simBA-5 read sets) with a k-mer LCA classifier that accepts single sequences, and you want both mates of a pair to vote together for one taxon. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-read-concatenation-for-classification))
- **paired-read-filtering-quality-control**: Use when preparing .bam files for inStrain profile/compare so that genome coverage, SNV calls, and strain-level comparisons (ConANI/popANI) rest on correctly mapped read pairs rather than spurious single-end or mis-oriented alignments. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-read-filtering-quality-control))
- **paired-read-merging-with-overlap-detection**: Use when paired-end Illumina read pairs overlap and must be converted into single merged fragments before downstream k-mer counting or seed construction, e.g. to derive 'not too repetitive' seeds for TULIP. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/paired-read-merging-with-overlap-detection))
- **paired-read-taxonomic-classification**: Use when paired-end read pairs (e.g. KAL_D, 26,114K read pairs) must be taxonomically assigned against multi-kingdom reference genome databases, especially when benchmarking MetaCache/AFS-MetaCache, CLARK, or Kraken2+Bracken classification quality or runtime. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-read-taxonomic-classification))
- **paired-replicate-differential-splicing-testing**: Use when RNA-seq count data provide per-allele exon inclusion and skipping counts (IJC/SJC) with incLen/skpLen effective lengths for individuals sharing a heterozygous SNP, and the goal is to call significant ASAS events at FDR &lt;= 10%. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-replicate-differential-splicing-testing))
- **paired-replicate-likelihood-ratio-testing**: Use when per-event allele-specific inclusion/skipping counts (IJC/SJC for REF and ALT alleles) are available for multiple individuals sharing a heterozygous SNP and you need a combined ASAS p-value via a paired-replicate LRT on δi = 0. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-replicate-likelihood-ratio-testing))
- **paired-replicate-statistical-model-fitting**: Use when you have per-allele exon inclusion (IJC) and skipping (SJC) counts plus effective isoform lengths (incLen, skpLen) from replicate RNA-seq samples sharing a heterozygous SNP, and need to test for differential splicing between alleles across replicates. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-replicate-statistical-model-fitting))
- **paired-review**: Run the paired review loop between an implementation session and a separate review session. Use once a feature branch is committed and before opening its pull request (change request), after pushing to an open PR, when asked to review a branch or PR as the paired review session, or when review findings arrive from another session. ([joshtsch/harness](https://github.com/joshtsch/harness/tree/HEAD/harness-skills/paired-review))
- **paired-reviewer**: Explicitly selected long-lived reviewer for the session you are working in: start one reviewer on another model, resume it every period to read this session's transcript and critique it against the user's own words, goals, time and money, and act on what holds up. Use when the user invokes $paired-reviewer or asks to pair a reviewer or evaluator with this session. Not the in-loop intent advocate you consult at decisions (intent-police), a one-shot independent opinion (fresh-consult), a code review, or the fleet watcher (agent-watcher). ([aelaguiz/arch_skill](https://github.com/aelaguiz/arch_skill/tree/HEAD/skills/paired-reviewer) · ★1)
- **paired-sample-matching-across-datasets**: Use when PDAC expression datasets contain patient-matched primary tumors, metastases, and/or derived cell lines (e.g., PACA-AU paired samples) and you need to compare signature scores across matched sample classes rather than across unrelated samples. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-sample-matching-across-datasets))
- **paired-scrnaseq-spatial-transcriptomics-benchmarking**: Use when comparing a spatial subpopulation identification method against baselines (Louvain, K-means, Hclust, SpaGCN, STAGATE, BayesSpace, DR-SC, scCoGAPS) on simulated data with known ground-truth subcluster labels, using ARI as the metric. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-scrnaseq-spatial-transcriptomics-benchmarking))
- **paired-scrnaseq-tcrseq-data-processing**: Use when you have paired single-cell transcriptome and full-length TCR sequencing data (e.g. Smart-seq2 CD8 TILs) and need per-cell clonotype assignments to test clonal relatedness between transcriptional states. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-scrnaseq-tcrseq-data-processing))
- **paired-session**: Use in exactly four cases - the user explicitly asks for paired-session, the review-loop entry hands off because .review-loop/config.md sets `entry: paired-session`, the review-loop entry hands off because that key is absent or invalid (the default entry), or the review-pr skill hands off on its paired route; with lifecycle on, this is the plan, implementation, independent-review, finish, polish, docs, security and acceptance workflow. Generic review-loop requests go to the review-loop entry first, which routes them here. ([NYTC69/review-loop](https://github.com/NYTC69/review-loop/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/paired-session) · ★3)
- **paired-session**: Drive an implementation through the paired-session coordinator: independent PLAN review, EXEC implementation/review, adversarial gate, then finish, quality polish, docs and security with lifecycle on, up to operator acceptance. Trigger in exactly five cases: (1) the user explicitly asks for paired-session or names this explicit coordinator entry; (2) the review-loop entry hands off because .review-loop/config.md sets `entry: paired-session`; (3) the review-loop entry hands off because that key is absent or invalid (the default entry); (4) /review-loop:review-pr hands off on its paired route (… ([NYTC69/review-loop](https://github.com/NYTC69/review-loop/tree/HEAD/skills/paired-session) · ★3)
- **paired-signature-estimate-statistical-comparison**: Use when the same tumor samples have signature-fitting estimates from two matched contexts (e.g., WES capture-restricted vs WGS SNV catalogs) and you must quantify per-sample systematic differences, such as exome-vs-WGS shifts for SBS9. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-signature-estimate-statistical-comparison))
- **paired-significance-testing-of-model-metrics**: Use when comparing two model-training conditions (e.g. augmentation on/off) evaluated on the same holdout test set across matched random seeds, and you must report statistically significant differences in loss or macro F1. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-significance-testing-of-model-metrics))
- **paired-statistical-significance-testing**: Use when two retrieval configurations are scored on the same set of queries and you must verify whether a reported difference (e.g. scaling from 1B to 27B parameters, or toggling database integration) is statistically supported. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-agentic-ai](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-agentic-ai/tree/HEAD/collections/agentic-ai/v1/leaves/paired-statistical-significance-testing))
- **paired-statistical-significance-testing**: Use when two or more methods have been evaluated on the same set of datasets and a per-dataset paired comparison of scores (e.g., AMI/NMI/ARI) must establish statistically significant ity rather than just a higher mean rank. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-statistical-significance-testing))
- **paired-strain-differential-expression**: Use when you have expression data from strains that carry a duplication paired with a non-duplicated strain of the same clonal population, and you need to test whether duplicated-region genes are induced and whether induction tracks copy number. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/paired-strain-differential-expression))
- **paired-t-test-cross-dataset-comparison**: Use when two hyperparameter-optimization or embedding-scoring methods must be compared across multiple matched datasets and per-dataset embedding-quality metrics (KNN/KNC preservation) need statistical significance testing via paired t-tests. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-t-test-cross-dataset-comparison))
- **paired-t-test-differential-expression**: Use when you have paired qPCR samples (e.g., 12 tumor/adjacent-normal breast cancer pairs) with raw CT values for a target miRNA and 2–3 internal controls, and you need to test differential expression under alternative reference-gene normalization schemes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-t-test-differential-expression))
- **paired-t-test-error-comparison**: Use when comparing classification error rates of two weighting schemes (e.g., uniform vs bespoke taxonomic weights) evaluated on the same test sequence variants and you need a paired significance test per taxon plus a per-phylum error-rate table. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/paired-t-test-error-comparison))
- **paired-t-test-evaluation**: Tests whether a peer prediction mechanism's scoring function is sensitive to report quality by verifying that replacing high-quality reports with degraded or LLM-generated low-quality reports leads to a statistically significant decrease in expected scores. Use when the user has predictions and gold and needs to compute paired difference t-test (p-value). ([qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool](https://github.com/qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool/tree/HEAD/skill-factory/output/paired-t-test-evaluation) · ★3)
- **paired-t-test-metabolite-screening**: Use when comparing replicate metabolite intensities measured on the same cultures at two time points (e.g. 0 h vs 72 h extracellular samples) and you must flag which metabolites show significant change before computing boundary fluxes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/paired-t-test-metabolite-screening))
- **paired-t-test-replicate-statistics**: Use when eCLIP/skipper reproducible_enriched_windows tables exist for matched WT and mutant replicates and you need to quantify region-proportion shifts (CDS, deep introns) or test differential enrichment (e.g., tRNA loci) with replicate-level statistics. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/paired-t-test-replicate-statistics))
- **paired-t-test-significance-testing**: Use when you have per-task performance values (e.g., Accuracy) for two methods evaluated on the same matched set of benchmark tasks and need to show one method's advantage is statistically significant, not just numerically higher. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/paired-t-test-significance-testing))
- **paired-t-test-significance-testing**: Use when comparing the clustering performance (AMI) of two methods evaluated on the same matched set of datasets or simulation replicates — e.g., 12 paired section-level AMI values — and you need a p-value for whether the improvement is statistically significant. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/paired-t-test-significance-testing))
- **paired-t-test-statistical-testing**: Use when two classification conditions (e.g., species-level with bespoke weights vs. genus-level with uniform weights) have been evaluated on the same matched test samples and you must test whether per-sample error rates or Bray-Curtis dissimilarities differ significantly. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/paired-t-test-statistical-testing))
- **paired-ttest-benchmark-comparison**: Use when two spectral-processing variants were applied to the same compounds/samples and you need a statistically grounded per-compound comparison of paired scores (e.g., matching scores with vs. without PerformDDADeconvolution). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/paired-ttest-benchmark-comparison))
- **paired-ttest-method-comparison**: Use when two or more methods are evaluated on the same datasets and you need a statistically grounded ranking: compute per-dataset metrics for every method, then run paired t-tests of the focal method against each baseline. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-ttest-method-comparison))
- **paired-wilcoxon-error-testing**: Use when two or more imputation methods have been run on the same masked datasets across repeated simulations (e.g. 10–30% MCAR values), yielding paired per-simulation error rates (NRMSE/PFC) that need significance testing against a reference method. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/paired-wilcoxon-error-testing))
- **paired-wilcoxon-rank-testing**: Use when comparing two related sets of continuous error scores (e.g., DLM vs. naive k-NN relative RT errors on the same validation molecules, or errors of molecules with vs. without a &gt;=threshold Tanimoto-similar training counterpart) and you need a distribution-free significance test. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/paired-wilcoxon-rank-testing))
- **paired-wilcoxon-signed-rank-testing**: Use when comparing a continuous per-sample score (e.g. signalling entropy SR from SCENT) between matched patient pairs, such as diagnostic–relapse AML bulk samples, and the score distribution is not assumed normal. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-wilcoxon-signed-rank-testing))
- **paired-wilcoxon-significance-testing**: Use when quantifying real-time depletion effects (e.g., RISER globin mRNA depletion in whole blood) and a null-comparison is needed against per-transcript percent changes arising from random splitting of an independent control run. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/paired-wilcoxon-significance-testing))
- **paired-wilcoxon-significance-testing**: Use when comparing paired per-fold performance metrics (Accuracy or macro F1-Score) of a MSMCE-integrated model vs. its baseline from 6-fold stratified cross-validation to establish statistical significance. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/paired-wilcoxon-significance-testing))
- **paired-wilcoxon-significance-testing**: Use when you have paired per-fold metric values (e.g., AMI, ARI, HOM, NMI from 5-fold cross-validation) for two methods on identical folds and need a one-sided significance test of ity. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-wilcoxon-significance-testing))
- **pairhatch**: Set up and explain the experimental Pairhatch broker for bounded, explicitly authorized agent consultations. ([JeffOtano/pairhatch](https://github.com/JeffOtano/pairhatch/tree/HEAD/skills/pairhatch))
- **pairing**: Build work collaboratively in reviewable pieces, handing each piece back for review before starting the next, so the person stays in the lead and steers while the work happens instead of reviewing a finished result. Use when someone says to pair with them on something, asks to collaborate rather than direct, wants to review as it goes, or wants to guide the work piece by piece — on code, on a design decision, or on writing. For a test-first build it runs tdd, for restructuring it runs refactor, for an interface contract it runs design-an-api, and for plan work it runs iterative-plan-review or… ([testdouble/han](https://github.com/testdouble/han/tree/HEAD/han-core/skills/pairing) · ★352 · 2 copies)
- **pairing**: User pairing, authentication, and trust management ([recogardtech/AutoPilotPM](https://github.com/recogardtech/AutoPilotPM/tree/HEAD/src/skills/bundled/pairing) · ★81 · 4 copies)
- **pairing**: Pair programming protocol where the agent drives and the user is the navigator. The pair agrees on a list of slices. Each cycle delivers one tested slice with intent, prediction, and a short why, and then stops for review. Use whenever the user wants to pair on code, says "let's pair", or mentions pair programming. Use also for a request to build step by step with a review between changes. ([IAmRadek/skills](https://github.com/IAmRadek/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pairing))
- **pairing**: Pair-program the active slice one small step at a time: agree the step, build it in the chosen mode (driver, navigator, ping-pong, dictation), prove it with a run, journal the why, commit on green. Also holds the memory bank formats every pair-* skill writes. Use when pairing on a slice, when the user says "let's pair," "you drive," "I'll write this one," "write exactly this," "ping-pong," or when another pair-* skill hands off to the loop. ([robjsliwa/skills](https://github.com/robjsliwa/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pairing/pairing))
- **pairing-buddy**: This skill should be used when the user says "let's start coding", "pair with me", "let's implement this", "open the plan", "pairing buddy", or "let's pair". Also trigger when starting a fresh session after architecture planning is complete and a plan exists in .claude/plans/. ([boundlesshq/groundwork](https://github.com/boundlesshq/groundwork/tree/HEAD/skills/pairing-buddy))
- **pairing-builder**: Interactive pairing creation from existing persona and role. Use when user wants to explore what emerges when a specific persona takes on a role. Triggers on "build a pairing", "create pairing", "pair persona with role", "what emerges when", "collision analysis". ([WillOsborn/digital-service-design-standards](https://github.com/WillOsborn/digital-service-design-standards/tree/HEAD/skills/pairing-builder) · ★3)
- **pairing-gears**: Guides software development one step at a time with configurable assistance levels from 1 to 5. Use when the user asks to be guided, coached, taught, paired with, helped step by step, allowed to implement the solution themselves, or asks what to do next during a coding task. Also use when the user wants to learn an unfamiliar codebase by working in it, or wants to code with minimal AI help to keep their skills sharp (for example a "no-AI day"). ([gianfrancodemarco/pairing-gears](https://github.com/gianfrancodemarco/pairing-gears/tree/HEAD/skills/pairing-gears) · ★2)

- **pairs-scanner**: Screen every pair in a basket for cointegration on daily closes and rank the tradeable ones by spread z-score. Runs the Engle-Granger two-step on log prices, tests the residual with a Dickey-Fuller t-stat against MacKinnon 2010 critical values, estimates the Ornstein-Uhlenbeck half-life of mean reversion, and flags out-of-sample regime shifts via a 70/30 residual std ratio. Emits per-pair hedge ratio, ADF t-stat and p-value bucket, half-life, current z-score, and a stability label. Use when a PM or stat-arb desk has a sector basket and asks "which two names are statistically tethered right no… ([rgourley/quant-garage](https://github.com/rgourley/quant-garage/tree/HEAD/skills/pairs-scanner) · ★7)

- **pairs-trading-project**: Build the pairs-trading portfolio project end-to-end: find two correlated stocks, test for cointegration, trade the z-score of their spread, backtest it leak-free, report full stats, and publish a GitHub repo. - **pairwise-causal-discovery-metric-analysis**: Use when per-experiment, per-model benchmark scores (e.g. C_detect/C_extract) are available and you must verify or reproduce reported Friedman chi-squared statistics and p-values across models for detection and extraction cases. - **pairwise-cell-distance-computation**: Use when a paired multimodal single-cell dataset (e.g., CITE-seq RNA counts + ADT antibody counts) needs a quantitative check that imputed RNA geometry matches protein-space geometry. - **pairwise-cell-transition-network-comparison**: Use when you have a longitudinal metabolomics matrix (e.g., NMR-derived concentrations across ordered developmental stages) and need to identify which metabolites or subnetworks are associated with each specific cell-state transition rather than the whole trajectory. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-cell-transition-network-comparison))
- **pairwise-celltype-comparison-statistics**: Use when per-cell-type isoform or TSS count tables (e.g. 'Modality-ModalityID.csv', NumModalityPerCluster) are available and the goal is to test whether one cell type (e.g. choroid plexus epithelium) shows distinctive isoform regulation versus another via pairwise DIE tests. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-chain-overlap-probability-computation))
- **pairwise-co-clustering-matrix-construction**: Use when two clustering solutions (e.g. SRIQ and consensus clustering) have been derived on the same sample set and you need a per-K quantitative similarity metric between their cluster partitions. - **pairwise-colocalization-baseline-comparison**: Use when benchmarking a colocalization or target-tissue prioritization method against a classical single-causal-variant pairwise baseline on loci with experimentally validated target genes/tissues. - **pairwise-comparative-metabolomics**: Use when you have centroided LC/MS data (mzML/mzXML) from two sample groups — e.g. hacl-1 vs. wildtype C. elegans, or 13C6-leucine-supplemented vs. unlabeled controls — and need to identify features enriched in one group or carrying isotope-label mass shifts. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-comparative-metabolomics))
- **pairwise-comparison-algorithm-reproduction**: Use when validating or reproducing a runtime/complexity benchmark between a reimplemented algorithm and its predecessor — specifically when timing Scoary2's pairwise-comparison code against original Scoary on synthetic gene-by-genome presence/absence matrices of varying dimensions. - **pairwise-complete-pearson-correlation-computation**: Use when computing gene-gene co-expression correlations from sparse, zero-inflated scRNA-seq count matrices where excess zeros make ordinary Pearson/Spearman correlations unreliable, and a confidence-filtered pairwise-complete estimate is needed. - **pairwise-compound-similarity-classification**: Use when you have FBMN/MSnLib annotation matches (top-10 matched compounds per query) as SMILES pairs and need to quantify and threshold their structural similarity via Tanimoto and MCES scores. - **pairwise-condition-cell-population-comparison**: Use when two processed, cell-type-annotated single-cell objects from distinct conditions are available and the goal is to identify which cell populations are enriched or depleted in one condition versus the other, e. - **pairwise-contrast-pvalue-computation**: Use when you have a wide-format feature table (features as rows, samples as columns) plus a design file mapping samples to treatment groups, and you need per-feature pairwise group contrast p-values and binary significance flags. - **pairwise-correlation-computation**: Use when you have a processed samples-by-metabolites table (post Viime imputation/normalization/transformation/scaling) and need to explore correlated behavior of metabolites, e.g. to build a metabolite network diagram whose edges encode signed correlation strength. Use when a delivery, account or recruiting manager asks "how are we doing on &lt;client&gt;'s roles?", "which roles need attention?", "which roles have gone quiet?", or wants a client roles report. Follows the pairwise-mcp skill. ([Pairwise-Technologies/pairwise-mcp-skills](https://github.com/Pairwise-Technologies/pairwise-mcp-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pairwise-delivery-client-roles-summary) · ★4)
- **pairwise-design-judging**: Judge competing UI and design candidates with blind, order-swapped pairwise comparisons and a Bradley–Terry fit instead of absolute 0–10 scores. Use when running design tournaments or iterative improve-and-judge loops with sub-agent designers and judges, when absolute judge scores saturate or tie, or when judges from different models score with different biases. ([ttulttul/pairwise-design-judging](https://github.com/ttulttul/pairwise-design-judging/tree/HEAD/skills/pairwise-design-judging))
- **pairwise-differential-gene-expression-analysis**: Use when a SingleCellExperiment of CD8 T cells has been assigned to reference states and you need gene signatures per state pair (or per state) to score/query cells or train a state classifier. - **pairwise-distance-correlation-evaluation**: Use when evaluating recovered 2D cell/spot coordinates from scRNA-seq against a spatial transcriptomics reference (Visium, MERSCOPE, MERFISH) where true test-set coordinates are known and methods like CeLEry, Tangram, spaOTsc, and novoSpaRc are being compared. - **pairwise-distance-matrix-construction**: Use when you have processed MS/MS spectra per LC-MS/MS feature and need a square pairwise spectral-similarity matrix as input for k-medoids clustering or feature-set testing in msFeaST. - **pairwise-distance-matrix-construction**: Use when you have a normalized scRNA-seq matrix with cell-type, condition, and patient metadata and need a cell-type-by-cell-type distance matrix (e.g., to cluster cell types by transcriptomic perturbation magnitude or lineage similarity). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pairwise-distance-matrix-construction))
- **pairwise-distance-matrix-tabulation**: Use when you have peak-listed LC-MS/MS (MGF) datasets for multiple samples and need a quantitative pairwise similarity/distance matrix without peptide identification or spectral annotation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-distance-matrix-tabulation))
- **pairwise-epigenome-local-alignment**: Use when two genomic regions are represented as single-track chromatin state sequences (ChromHMM labels) and you need a local alignment score to find which regions/gene bodies are epigenomically similar to a query region. - **pairwise-feature-correlation-analysis**: Use when a Compound Discoverer (v3.3) peak table retains adduct, dimer, or in-source-fragment features co-eluting with identified lipids and pairwise correlation filtering is needed to reduce spurious unidentified peaks. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-feature-correlation-analysis))
- **pairwise-genetic-distance-calculation**: Use when you have FASTA consensus sequences per specimen/marker (e.g. Nanopore rDNA consensus and Illumina reference consensus, or COI barcodes vs rDNA regions) and need a pairwise distance matrix partitioned into intra- vs interspecific classes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/pairwise-genetic-distance-calculation))
- **pairwise-genome-ani-comparison**: Use when you need sensitive strain-level comparison of microbial populations across metagenomic samples, e.g. testing whether related individuals share more strains than unrelated ones. - **pairwise-group-comparison-statistics**: Use when a pre-processed (missing-value-handled, log-transformed, pareto-scaled) targeted or untargeted metabolomics feature table must be compared across sample groups to identify features with statistically significant and practically relevant abundance changes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-group-comparison-statistics))
- **pairwise-group-methylation-comparison**: Use when you have a clustered-region methylation counts table (combined.csv with chr, start, end, CpG and per-sample -N/-X count columns) and need to identify DMRs between two or more sample groups. - **pairwise-group-significance-testing-of-beta-diversity**: Use when a rarefied OTU/feature table and sample metadata are available and you need an ordination plot plus statistical p-values for every pairwise group comparison of community composition differences. - **pairwise-instrain-profile-comparison**: Use when pairwise genomic comparison of the same genome set across metagenomic samples must distinguish true shared strains from co-occurring but divergent populations, e.g. twin strain-sharing benchmarks. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/pairwise-instrain-profile-comparison))
- **pairwise-interaction-agreement-statistics**: Use when two independent methods (e.g., SMETANA and COMMIT) have predicted metabolite exchanges over the same gap-filled community member models and you need to quantify how much their pairwise interaction predictions agree, including shared metabolites. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-interaction-agreement-statistics))
- **pairwise-interaction-eval**: Evaluates a robot's ability to detect interacting human pairs and classify their coarse-grained interaction types (e.g., walking, standing, sitting together) using bounding box geometry and optical flow, without relying on costly skeleton-based pose estimation. Use when the user wants to benchmark on JRDB, Collective Activity Dataset (CAD), Lawnmower Dataset, or asks about evaluating this task. Reports Accuracy. ([qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool](https://github.com/qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool/tree/HEAD/skill-factory/output/pairwise-interaction-eval) · ★3)
- **pairwise-local-alignment-score-computation**: Use when the goal is to score similarity between genomic regions represented as chromatin state sequences (e.g. 500 kb Roadmap query regions vs. other regions or randomized negative controls) with a local alignment algorithm. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/pairwise-local-alignment-score-computation))
- **pairwise-locus-level-correlation-computation**: Use when evaluating BIRD predictions across multiple cell types: you have predicted and true DH matrices for ≥3 cell types and need pair-wise locus-level accuracy of differential DH, stratified by cell-type similarity. - **pairwise-ma-methodology**: Deep methodology knowledge for pairwise meta-analysis including fixed vs random effects, heterogeneity assessment, publication bias, and sensitivity analysis. - **pairwise-mass-comparison-computation**: Use when after peak m/z values and molecular formulas have been extracted and pre-processed from FT-ICR MS data, and you need to reconstruct biochemical transformation networks ab initio. - **pairwise-mass-difference-computation**: Use when after loading preprocessed MSI intensity data (via msimat from CSV export) or a simple numeric vector of mass peak values, when you need to discover which masses in your dataset co-vary as parent–adduct pairs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/pairwise-mass-difference-computation))
- **pairwise-mass-difference-histogram-analysis**: Use when you have peak-mass lists from MALDI-MSI datasets (e.g. SCiLS/MSiReader CSV exports or peakBinned Cardinal imzML) and need to find which peaks are matrix- or salt-adduct partners of parent ions. Der Weise zeigt alle erreichbaren Pakete (installiert, aus bekannten Katalogen, aus Ordnern) zur Auswahl, prueft jede Datei per Pruefsumme, fragt nach dem Lernziel der Person, legt das Thema im Werkraum an (mit Python auch im Speicher) und tauscht bei Updates nur das Paketmaterial.
